User: It is now 11/3/2026. A new dev day has started. I've been testing locally and have been really enjoying the ringed nodes on the 3D local map as eye-catching elements that can expand into more. The alternation between full and isolated structure is honestly working better than I envisioned as well. Still, lots of work remains within the totality of the Live Documentation Explorer. Can you re-read yesterday's chat in full alongside the summaries you pick up just so you can check to make sure all the ideas and exchanges got integrated into our workspace docs appropriately? After that, what would you like to work on next? It might be wise to give the software a spin and click around for a bit to see what you do and don't like. (I've set the model to Astra, which I've noted as having the current most human-like vision capabilities). 

Codex: I’ll read the workspace memory, the five latest summaries, and yesterday’s full conversation, then check that the ideas and decisions made it into the docs. After that I’ll explore the running UI and recommend what to work on next, using the vision’s priorities and what I see in the browser.

No product implementation changed.' && git status --short



I read yesterday’s full export and maintained record, the five latest summaries, the narrative and Memory. The substantive ideas were preserved, but I corrected several integration mistakes:

- Five provenance notes had landed inside generation timestamps, visibly corrupting the reader’s date field.
- The Explorer docs mixed the old starting rules with the new island-based start.
- Two decisions sat under the wrong headings, and the summary overstated one failed test run.

Your positive verdict on rings and full/local switching is now recorded.

**I’d work next on the 2D journey: find a symbol, understand it, then follow its connections.** Trying it exposed a concrete sequence worth improving:

1. **Keep identities exact.** `Walk` and `walk` still get conflated.
2. **Land on what was requested.** Choosing a symbol through search left its row and controls partly outside a 1280×800 viewport.
3. **Read without losing the picture.** Opening Details can cover the very card being explained. Your idea of revealing existing Purpose and symbol documentation on zoom has a clear job here.

The rings make expansion inviting, and the smaller estate map already communicates relationships well. I’d preserve those strengths while improving this journey. Moving authored prose into source comments remains a separate design fork.

The `review contains six screenshots and reproductions`. Documentation checks, regeneration checks, the rebuild and focused browser verification passed. Committed as `44a505bf`; the workspace is clean.

User: 1 and 2 sound straightforward. 3 is going to require a few design propsals (visual layout proposals). We'll need to try several different things to make sure that looks okay across a very wide variety of nodes with a wide variety of comment lengths (including absences). 

Codex: I’ll fix symbol identity and search framing first. For on-map explanations, I’ll prepare several visual proposals using the same varied set of real files and symbols—missing comments, short summaries, long prose and structured documentation—so we can compare both appearance and navigation before choosing a design.

The identity loss starts before drawing: the Explorer strips the distinctions already present in the docs, then its matching code collapses them again. I’ll preserve those distinctions through the graph projection, pins, wires and reader. For framing, I’ll make search target the requested row and its controls, and account for the layout movement that follows the selection.

 {label:'One public type · long file notes',file:'packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/controller.ts',symbol:'LocalViewController',note:'One exposed type with substantial authored notes. Try File notes.'},
 {label:'No public symbols · authored explanation',file:'packages/explorer/src/client/index.ts',note:'The file has a Purpose and long Notes, but no public symbol list.'},
 {label:'Many undocumented IDs · HTML',file:'packages/explorer/src/shared/template.html',symbol:'search-subject',note:'140 documented element IDs with little symbol prose.'},
 {label:'Few symbols · very long file notes',file:'packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphView.ts',symbol:'createForceGraphView',note:'The longest authored block in the sample set. Try File notes.'},

const query=new URLSearchParams(location.search);
let chosen=Math.max(0,Math.min(cases.length-1,Number(query.get('"'case'))||0));
let mode=['all','one','beside'].includes(query.get('mode')||'')?query.get('mode')"'!:'"'all';
let file:FileDoc;let files:Record<string,FileDoc>;let selected:string|undefined;
let provider:FileDoc|undefined;let consumer:FileDoc|undefined;
const bodyOf=(s:SymbolDoc|undefined)=>s?.sections.map(x=>"'`### ${x.title}'"\\n"'${x.body.join('"'\\n')}"'`).join('"'\\n\\n')||'';
const summaryOf=(s:SymbolDoc)=>s.sections.find(x=>x.title==='Summary')?.body.join('\\n')||'';
const prose=(s:string)=>renderMarkdown(s,{linkHandler:(_href,text)=>"'`<span class="prose-link">${text}</span>`});
function authored(heading:string){const value=authoredSections(file.authored).find(s=>s.heading?.toLowerCase()===heading.toLowerCase())?.markdown.replace(/''^###[''^'"\\n]*\\n?/,'').trim()||'';return /"'^_?Pending(?: authored purpose| notes)?_?'"\\.?"'$/i.test(value)?'"'':value;}
function button(name:string,cls='',action?:()=>void){const e=document.createElement('button');e.type='button';e.className=cls;e.textContent=name;if(action)e.addEventListener('click',action);return e;}
function save(){const q=new URLSearchParams({case:String(chosen),mode,detail:zoom.value});if(selected)q.set('symbol',selected);if(notes.checked)q.set('notes','1');history.replaceState(null,'','?'+q);}
function selectSymbol(name:string|undefined){selected=name;subjects.value=name||'';render(true);}
function fileHeader(node:FileDoc,isMain=false){const head=document.createElement('div');head.className='file-head';const name=node.codePath.split('/').at(-1)"'!;head.append(button(name,'"'',isMain?()=>selectSymbol(undefined):undefined));const path=document.createElement('small');path.textContent=node.codePath;head.append(path);for(const side of ['in','out']){const port=document.createElement('i');port.className='file-port '+side;head.append(port);}return head;}
function contextCard(node:FileDoc|undefined,side:string){if("'!node)return;const e=document.createElement('"'article');e.className='card context '+side;e.append(fileHeader(node));const body=document.createElement('div');body.className='body';body.innerHTML="'`<div>${node.symbols.length} public ${node.symbols.length===1?'"'symbol':'symbols'}</div><div class=\"count\">"'${side==='"'provider'?'Used by this file':'Uses this file'} · "'${side==='"'provider'?file.outbound.length:file.inbound.length} "'${side==='"'provider'?'providers':'consumers'} in all</div>"'`;e.append(body);cards.append(e);return e;}
function render(preserve=false){
 const old=preserve?cards.querySelector<HTMLElement>('"'.symbol.selected .row, .subject .file-head')?.getBoundingClientRect():null;
 cards.replaceChildren();const reading=Number(zoom.value)>=1.2;document.querySelector('#zoom-value')"'!.textContent=reading?'"'Reading':'Overview';
 for(const b of document.querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>('[data-mode]'))b.setAttribute('aria-pressed',String(b.dataset.mode===mode));
 const primary=document.createElement('article');primary.className='card subject '+mode;primary.append(fileHeader(file,true));
 const purpose=authored('Purpose'),fileNotes=notes.checked?authored('Notes'):'';
 const block=(text:string,cls:string)=>{const e=document.createElement('div');e.className=cls+' prose';e.innerHTML=prose(text);return e;};
 if(reading&&mode"'!=='"'beside'&&purpose)primary.append(block(purpose,'purpose'));
 if(reading&&mode"'!=='"'beside'&&"'!selected&&fileNotes)primary.append(block('"'### Notes\\n'+fileNotes,'notes'));
 for(const symbol of file.symbols){const section=document.createElement('section');section.className='symbol'+(symbol.name===selected?' selected':'');section.dataset.symbol=symbol.name;const row=document.createElement('div');row.className='row';row.innerHTML='<i class=\"dot\"></i>';row.append(button(symbol.name,'',()=>selectSymbol(symbol.name)));const kind=document.createElement('span');kind.className='kind';kind.textContent=symbol.kind;row.append(kind);const offer=document.createElement('i');offer.className='dot offer';row.append(offer);section.append(row);
   if(reading&&mode"'!=='"'beside'&&(mode==='all'||symbol.name===selected)){const text=symbol.name===selected?bodyOf(symbol):summaryOf(symbol);if(text)section.append(block(text,'symbol-detail'));}
   primary.append(section);
 }
 if("'!file.symbols.length){const empty=document.createElement('"'div');empty.className='empty-symbols';empty.textContent='File-level connections';primary.append(empty);}
 if(reading&&mode"'!=='"'beside'&&selected&&fileNotes)primary.append(block('### Notes\\n'+fileNotes,'notes'));
 cards.append(primary);const left=contextCard(provider,'provider'),right=contextCard(consumer,'consumer');
 const selectedDoc=file.symbols.find(s=>s.name===selected);const currentText=selected?bodyOf(selectedDoc):purpose;
 let readingCard:HTMLElement|undefined;
 if(reading&&mode==='beside'&&(currentText||fileNotes)){readingCard=document.createElement('article');readingCard.className='card reading';const head=document.createElement('div');head.className='read-head';head.innerHTML="'`<strong>${esc(selected||file.codePath.split('"'/').at(-1)"'!)}</strong><small>${esc(selectedDoc?.kind||'"'file')}</small>"'`;readingCard.append(head);const content=block(currentText+(fileNotes?'"'\\n\\n### Notes\\n'+fileNotes:''),'read-body');content.tabIndex=0;content.setAttribute('aria-label','Documentation');readingCard.append(content);cards.append(readingCard);}
 const width=reading?440:310;primary.style.width=width+'px';const hasAside="'!!readingCard;const primaryX=Math.max(342,(viewport.clientWidth-width-(hasAside?385:0))/2);primary.style.left=primaryX+'"'px';primary.style.top='66px';
 if(left){left.style.left='30px';left.style.top='110px';}
 if(right){right.style.left=(primaryX+width+(hasAside?405:100))+'px';right.style.top='200px';}
 if(readingCard){readingCard.style.left=(primaryX+width+24)+'px';const row=primary.querySelector<HTMLElement>('.symbol.selected .row');readingCard.style.top=Math.max(66,(row?.getBoundingClientRect().top||primary.getBoundingClientRect().top)-scene.getBoundingClientRect().top-15)+'px';}
 const extent=[...cards.children].map(e=>(e as HTMLElement).getBoundingClientRect());const origin=scene.getBoundingClientRect();scene.style.width=Math.max(viewport.clientWidth,...extent.map(r=>r.right-origin.left+40))+'px';scene.style.height=Math.max(viewport.clientHeight,...extent.map(r=>r.bottom-origin.top+70))+'px';drawWires();
 document.querySelector('#case-note')"'!.textContent=cases[chosen].note;
 const comments=file.symbols.filter(s=>s.sections.length).length;document.querySelector('"'#counts')"'!.textContent=`${file.symbols.length} symbols · ${comments} with comments · ${currentText.length.toLocaleString()} selected characters`;
 if(old){const now=primary.querySelector<HTMLElement>('"'.symbol.selected .row')?.getBoundingClientRect()||primary.querySelector('.file-head')"'!.getBoundingClientRect();viewport.scrollTop+=now.top-old.top;}
 save();
}
function drawWires(){const sceneBox=scene.getBoundingClientRect();const point=(selector:string)=>{const r=cards.querySelector(selector)?.getBoundingClientRect();return r?{x:r.x+r.width/2-sceneBox.left,y:r.y+r.height/2-sceneBox.top}:null;};
 wires.replaceChildren();wires.setAttribute('"'viewBox',"'`0 0 ${scene.scrollWidth} ${scene.scrollHeight}`);wires.style.width=scene.scrollWidth+'"'px';wires.style.height=scene.scrollHeight+'px';
 const wire=(a:ReturnType<typeof point>,b:ReturnType<typeof point>,reading=false)=>{if("'!a||!b)return;const p=document.createElementNS('"'http://www.w3.org/2000/svg','path');const dx=Math.max(50,Math.abs(b.x-a.x)*.45);p.setAttribute('d',"'`M${a.x},${a.y} C${a.x+dx},${a.y} ${b.x-dx},${b.y} ${b.x},${b.y}`);p.setAttribute('"'fill','none');p.setAttribute('stroke',reading?'#8da9be':'#299da8');p.setAttribute('stroke-width',reading?'1.5':'2');if(reading)p.setAttribute('stroke-dasharray','4 5');wires.append(p);};
 wire(point('.provider .file-port.out'),point('.subject .file-port.in'));wire(point('.subject .file-port.out'),point('.consumer .file-port.in'));
 const side=cards.querySelector('.reading')?.getBoundingClientRect();if(side){const a=point('.symbol.selected .dot.offer')||point('.subject .file-port.out');wire(a,{x:side.left-sceneBox.left,y:side.top-sceneBox.top+25},true);}
}
async function load(){const c=cases[chosen];const base=c.base||'';if("'!cache.has(base)){const d=await fetch(base+'"'/explorer-data.json').then(r=>r.json());cache.set(base,d.graph.files);}files=cache.get(base)"'!;file=Object.values(files).find(f=>f.codePath===c.file)!;if(!file)throw new Error('"'Missing actual file: '+c.file);selected=(chosen===Number(query.get('case')||0)?query.get('symbol'):undefined)||c.symbol; if("'!file.symbols.some(s=>s.name===selected))selected=undefined;provider=files[file.outbound.find(id=>id!==file.codePath)!];consumer=files[file.inbound.find(id=>id!==file.codePath)!];subjects.replaceChildren();subjects.add(new Option('"'File purpose', ''));for(const s of file.symbols)subjects.add(new Option(s.name,s.name));subjects.value=selected||'';const link=document.querySelector<HTMLAnchorElement>('#explorer')"'!;link.href=base+'"'/?view=local&mode=2d&node='+encodeURIComponent(file.codePath);viewport.scrollTop=0;viewport.scrollLeft=0;render();}
for(const [i,c]of cases.entries())select.add(new Option(c.label,String(i)));select.value=String(chosen);zoom.value=query.get('detail')||'1.3';notes.checked=query.get('notes')==='1';select.addEventListener('change',()=>{chosen=Number(select.value);void load();});subjects.addEventListener('change',()=>selectSymbol(subjects.value||undefined));for(const b of document.querySelectorAll<HTMLButtonElement>('[data-mode]'))b.addEventListener('click',()=>{mode=b.dataset.mode"'!;render(true);});zoom.addEventListener('"'input',()=>render(true));notes.addEventListener('change',()=>render(true));window.addEventListener('resize',()=>{if(file)render(true);});viewport.addEventListener('wheel',event=>{if(event.ctrlKey){event.preventDefault();zoom.value=String(Math.max(.7,Math.min(1.6,Number(zoom.value)-Math.sign(event.deltaY)*.1)));render(true);}},{passive:false});

Completed with input: return (await page.screenshot()).toString('base64');

User: What if the description of a node only appeared when it was tapped or hovered over? What if the description of a symbol only appeared when it was tapped or hovered over? We'd need the cards to expand and contract a bit at will, but what do you think about that?

Codex: Yes—that seems more promising than showing prose just because there’s room for it. Hover could briefly reveal a file’s Purpose or a symbol’s summary; tapping could keep it open, with a second tap closing it. Pinning would remain a separate action, and missing descriptions would cause no expansion.

The crucial part is keeping the hovered title or symbol steady while the card grows. Otherwise expansion can move the target away from the pointer and trigger a collapse/reopen loop. I’ll add this as a comparison, including short hover intent versus immediate opening. It would change today’s tap-to-open-Details behavior, so I’ll keep that change in the probe for now.

The expanding-card idea has a useful advantage: it keeps the explanation beside the thing being read without making every card permanently taller. I’m trying a compact resting state, a temporary hover preview, and a tap that holds the description open. The main comparison will be how much nearby content moves, especially when moving between symbols with very different comment lengths.


Ran terminal command: kill -TERM -- -63780



The prototype now includes “On attention,” with both immediate hover and a short delay to compare. Empty descriptions stay compact, and longer structured documentation has a separate expansion.

The repair checks passed unit and integration tests. During review I found one additional identity edge case: a symbol name can itself match another symbol’s anchor. I’m tightening that conversion before the final browser run.

### Continued implementation and probe work

- The third gate passed 1,558 unit tests and 115 integration tests. Root review then found a second interpretation of already-resolved source headings in the projection's link helper: an explicit anchor can equal a different symbol's heading. The gate was deliberately stopped before browser execution, using only its verified process group 63780, to correct that case. Resolution now happens only at anchor-bearing inputs, and a regression gives one declaration's anchor the exact name of another. All current projected main/estate link endpoints already resolved to existing headings (4,913 and 95 links respectively); the new case protects a valid custom-anchor input absent from those samples. A stale controller comment about slugged edges was corrected too. The next gate must verify this final source.
- Added the fourth disposable mode, On attention: stable DOM, file/symbol descriptions absent until hover or tap, 300 ms versus immediate hover, transient preview versus a tapped open description, and an explicit Full documentation expansion beyond the Summary. An open description remains while the pointer reads it; an explicitly held description is not replaced by brushing another row. Keyboard focus previews and Escape closes. This is a proposal, not an approved change to Details or the production graph layout. Root visual evaluation remains pending.

### Assistant — further delivered commentary

"'```text
The expanding-card idea has a useful advantage: it keeps the explanation beside the thing being read without making every card permanently taller. I’m trying a compact resting state, a temporary hover preview, and a tap that holds the description open. The main comparison will be how much nearby content moves, especially when moving between symbols with very different comment lengths.

# Exact symbols and search arrival

Historical Explorer review, November 3, 2026, following the repairs authorised in [Turn 2](../../../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md#turn-2). Captured in standalone Chromium at 1280×800 from the built Explorer on the owner's existing port 8900. The root inspected all three pictures. The layout used its normal continuing search, not a frozen test-only layout.

| Picture | Journey and evidence |
| --- | --- |
| [Neighbours after search](neighbours-search-1280.png) | Retain the configuration file, then search for Neighbours. The requested row and its actions land inside the usable map. Measured label x=623, y=386.99, width=231, height=12, compared with the earlier right-edge clipping documented in the parent review. |
| [Neighbours on hover](neighbours-hover-1280.png) | Its direct partners are reachFrom, components and walk (function). Walk (interface) remains unrelated to this hover; the heading distinction survives projection, disclosure and drawing. |

Yes—after trying it, this is my preferred direction:

- Hover or keyboard focus reveals the description.
- Tap holds it open; another tap or Escape closes it.
- Missing descriptions add no space.
- Full Notes and examples require an explicit action.

The crucial constraint is keeping the acted-on name stationary while the card changes.

I added **On attention** to the [live comparison](http://127.0.0.1:8900/probes/reading/?case=0&mode=attention&detail=1.3): four proposals, twelve real examples. It remains a prototype.

Ran terminal command: "cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md <<'EOF'

### Probe implementation checkpoint

- The new ignored preview is under "'`dist/explorer/probes/pinned-reading/`. It imports the production card factory, exact graph projection, pin-state helpers, markdown renderer and shared-URL codec, and copies the current theme/card/Local Map CSS for the disposable page. It adds inline reading slots to the real card DOM; file and symbol pin policies are independent controls, and summaries/full symbol docs are a separate content-pressure switch. Rules change without replacing pins or card DOM. Names retain an explicit full-document reader.
- This comparison uses the shipped pin state machine: a whole-file pin narrows to a symbol, and removing the last symbol pin leaves a whole-file pin. That interaction can make the “file plus all symbols” policy expand more prose on a last-symbol release; preserve it for evaluation rather than changing retention semantics to make a picture look quieter. There is one actual provider and consumer where available, de-duplicated, alongside the subject; cards have fixed origins and the SVG redraws against their real row anchors during slot transitions. This is a fixed-scope comparison, not the full native map layout/router.
- Hover in this new comparison retains the existing wire-attention behavior; pin changes control geometry. It does not claim to fix or select the old attention-only interaction. Cyclic pin/policy checks, visual inspection, narrow-screen review and data-absence cases remain to perform. The old attention probe'"'s cumulative offset bug remains recorded as a rejected prototype behavior.

### Assistant — delivered scope commentary

"'```text
In this comparison, pins will control expansion and hover will use the familiar wire highlighting. That gives us a steady card position while we judge the four pin rules. I’ll reuse the actual card renderer, pin controls and typography, and keep the file-click idea recorded as a separate open choice.
```
EOF'

## Owner feedback, later November 3

In [Turn 4](../../../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md#turn-4), the owner reports that repeated hovering, particularly alternating Walk and walk while moving down the list, walks the card down the screen. The [supplied picture](../pinned-reading/owner-hover-drift.png) preserves that state. The prototype's row-holding offsets accumulate; the earlier zero-displacement checks covered individual switches, not a full interaction cycle returning to the same card geometry. That limited check did not establish freedom from drift.

The owner is comfortable with hover expansion as an idea, but calls this implementation rough compared with the existing 2D Local Map. They request a further throwaway comparing what whole-file and symbol pins reveal. Hover/tap-only attention remains an open design direction; this prototype is not an accepted implementation. The next [pin-driven comparison](../pinned-reading/README.md) retains this feedback and tests the pin-policy permutations.
EOF
cat > AI-Agent-Workspace/Probes/2026-11-03/pinned-reading/README.md <<'EOF'
# Pin-driven descriptions

Historical design probe, November 3, 2026. Requested in [Turn 4](../../../ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md#turn-4). This is a disposable comparison under the ignored Explorer output, not a selected product behavior.

The owner reports cumulative downward drift in the previous attention proposal and finds its presentation rough beside the native 2D map. [Their supplied screenshot](owner-hover-drift.png) is copied unchanged from the attachment and is evidence of that report. They accept hover expansion as an idea and ask for another throwaway in which pins expand descriptions, with both rules left open.

## The four combinations

| File pin | Symbol pin |
| --- | --- |
| File description | Symbol description |
| File description | Symbol and file descriptions |
| File and all symbol descriptions | Symbol description |
| File and all symbol descriptions | Symbol and file descriptions |

The two controls change independently over the same case and pin set. A third control compares summaries with full structured symbol documentation; the file description is its authored Purpose. Names open a reader for the full documentation, including Notes. The file-click-to-expand-all suggestion remains a separate open idea.

## Implementation and scope

The preview imports the real [card renderer](../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/localView/card-factory.ts), [pin state machine](../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/pin-state.ts), [connection geometry](../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/connection-geometry.ts), graph projection and Markdown renderer. It uses the current native theme/card/Local Map styles, with reading-slot layout and modest font/spacing adjustments. Existing pin semantics are preserved: a whole-file pin narrows to a symbol, and releasing the last symbol leaves a whole-file pin. The chip tray can release a pin completely. This distinction matters when a file pin is set to reveal every description.

Cards have fixed origins, and reading slots grow downward. Hover highlights actual references and does not control geometry in this comparison. This isolates the requested pin-policy question; it does not repair or choose the earlier hover-only design. The fixed scope is the chosen file with one actual provider and consumer where available, de-duplicated. Wires follow actual symbol anchors and update during slot transitions; self references use the native lace geometry. The full directory-aware layout and router are not reproduced here.

Twelve real-content cases cover the same short/long/absent/structured and file-shape pressures as the [earlier comparison](../reading-layouts/README.md#common-cases). Missing descriptions add no placeholder text or empty slots. Policy and pin state survive sharing/reload. The built page and its editable source live under ignored "'`dist/explorer/probes/pinned-reading/` while it is under review; neither is committed.

## Evaluation status

Initial visual inspection found and corrected content-width symbol rows (the native grid'"'s centering had survived a switch to flex rows), and replaced the first generic self-reference routes with the native laces. The initial four policy states rendered without page errors. Further visual review, repeated interaction cycles, touch/keyboard and absence/long-text cases are in progress; no acceptance verdict is claimed yet.
EOF

Two independent controls give all four combinations:

- **File pin:** file description, or file plus every symbol description.
- **Symbol pin:** symbol description, or symbol plus file description.

Changing rules preserves your pins. **Text** switches between summaries and full comments across twelve real examples.

User: Not confident in the choice to bring up a text modal on click, but I overall like the behavior. Let's default to a file pin causing the file and all symbol descriptions to expand until we (probably quickly) hit a wall which makes that nearly impossible due to the overwhelming size of some node or other and all its symbols. 

I still want to be able to have symbol descriptions appear on hover and file description appear on hover on a node without hovering over its symbol rows. 

If we find that the average case for file pinning is too noisy, we can shrink back down to file description only expanding. 

Codex: I’ll make file pinning expand the file description and all symbol descriptions, with file-description-only kept as the fallback you described. I’ll also restore both hover disclosures: a symbol’s description over its row, and the file description over the card’s non-symbol area.

I’ll keep click-to-modal out of the next iteration and check repeated hover transitions explicitly, so the card cannot accumulate the offsets that caused the walking.

### Work notes

- This steers the active Turn 5 iteration: compare both name-click actions in the prototype rather than choosing one for the owner. File-pin-all remains the selected initial default; both requested hover disclosures remain required. The previous modal is removed from name clicks.
- Hover implementation hypothesis: keep the bounded set of descriptions revealed during the current card visit, then remove its unpinned previews on leaving (with short intent/exit grace). A symbol-list gap does not trigger file Purpose; header/path/padding does. Geometry-generated row boundary events must not create new reading targets. This is an explicit prototype motion choice, not an adopted production policy. Test long-to-short and adjacent-row journeys, not only the resting end state.

User: Oof, here's a tricky UX question: is there ever a case where a user might wish to share with their colleagues a view in which a card is fully expanded without pinning it? Is ther ever a case where a user might wish to share a view with pinned nodes/symbols but hidden descriptions? 

Codex: Yes. A walkthrough could show an unpinned provider’s full explanation beside a pinned caller, without adding the provider’s whole neighborhood. A dependency overview could keep several files and symbols pinned while folding all prose for a cleaner picture.

That suggests **pins and reading state should be independently shareable**. Pinning can still open descriptions by default, while explicit open/close choices override that default. Hover previews would remain temporary.

This gives the separate-reading alternative a stronger purpose. I’ll record these scenarios alongside the comparison; the click choice still needs your hands-on judgment.

Yes. Both seem useful:

- **Expanded, unpinned:** a walkthrough showing a provider’s full explanation beside a pinned caller, without bringing in the provider’s entire neighborhood.
- **Pinned, descriptions hidden:** a dependency discussion where several retained files and symbols define the picture, and prose would overwhelm the connections.

I’d make **retention and reading independently shareable**. Your file-pin default can still open every description initially; a subsequent deliberate collapse should win and survive sharing. Likewise, explicitly opened descriptions should survive without requiring a pin. Hover alone stays temporary.

This strengthens the case for an independent **expand/fold all descriptions on this card** action, whichever name-click behavior wins. We would also need to preserve an expanded unpinned card’s visibility when restoring the shared view.

Both click modes are now in the [live comparison](http://127.0.0.1:8900/probes/pinned-reading/). It already shares individual reading overrides separately from pins; the single whole-card reading action remains to prototype. Repeated hover, keyboard, touch and reload checks passed.

User: Pretty solid! I'm feeling it out and:
- the delay before expanding is a little much, 
- the delay before collapsing also feels a little odd (if you hover over a symbol directly under the last hover-expanded symbol, the first does not collapse until the mouse is sufficiently far away as to trigger the collapse of the second, based on my testing)

Nevertheless, I think this is really cool. The only other thing we haven't attempted to solve yet is how we want text and _zooming_ to relate to one another, as the user is permitted to zoom in and out great distances in the 2D Local Map. We also should consider how we ensure reasonable click semantic equivalence throughout the app as we try to nail down the controls optimized for typical user flows through the application. 

This probe was a worthy effort, and I think we've found a sufficiently good baseline combination of elements that we can begin integrating into Live Documentation (unless you feel otherwise, and please voice so if you feel that way). As always, if there are any design forks you need  my eyes on, bring it to my attention. Thank you for your continued efforts. 



Codex: I agree this is a useful baseline to integrate. Your collapse observation is accurate: the probe deliberately keeps earlier descriptions open until you leave the card. That prevented the drift, but made hover feel sticky; simply shortening the timer won’t fix it.

I’ll trace how reading state would fit the real layout, zoom, sharing and existing click actions, then bring back the concrete forks with recommendations. The integration needs to preserve your bearings while descriptions open and close, and the controls need to make sense beyond this one view.

### Work notes

- The owner authorizes beginning integration of the baseline, gives positive feedback, rejects the excessive hover delay and describes the sticky visit behavior, and explicitly calls out zoom/text and application-wide click semantics for design. This does not choose one of the two name-click modes or resolve semantic zoom. Establish the actual integration surface and give concrete recommendations at those forks before building their dependent behavior. Routine read-only tracing and integration planning proceed without another permission request.

The existing app has a real click mismatch: 3D file names retain or release their neighborhood, while 2D names open the reader. I lean toward making map-name clicks retain consistently, with a separate reading control that can expand or fold descriptions independently.

For zoom, I lean toward folding prose in the overview and restoring deliberate reading choices when you zoom back in. Keeping paragraphs readable at every zoom would make them cover the map; scaling them with everything else produces tiny text. Those two choices need your judgment before I build their behavior.

For the integrated Local Map, which primary-click rule should we use? 3D names currently retain/release a file; 2D names currently open its reader.
- Map names pin/unpin in both perspectives; a separate reading control opens or folds text (recommended).
- 2D names toggle inline reading; 3D names keep their current retain/release action.

How should deliberately opened descriptions behave when zooming far out in the 2D map? Their open/closed choices would remain saved in either case.
- Fold prose for the overview and restore it at reading zoom, with an anchored transition (recommended).
- Keep descriptions expanded and scale them with their cards, preserving the expanded layout.

### Integration trace checkpoint

- Read the controller, pan/zoom, branch renderer/motion, compressed URL and retained-state Live Docs in full, then inspected the corresponding implementation boundaries. The inspect CLI's inbound walk confirms the card factory feeds both the Local Map controller and branch renderer, through the Local Map entry to the application root. The implementation still receives canonical GraphFiles in the application for the reader, while the render payload intentionally contains identities/type references rather than a second copy of prose.
- The 2D zoom is 0.05–3 and is a transform of the entire card layer. At the probe's 13 px prose size that would range from 0.65 to 39 screen pixels. The root recommends a presentation-level overview/reading threshold that preserves intentional open choices, with different entry/exit thresholds to prevent flicker, an anchored transition and no perspective change. This is a recommendation pending the owner's word, not a chosen threshold or production implementation.
- Actual click contracts: 2D file/symbol names call inspection without retaining; 3D file names/spheres call the reversible shared retention action; World Map hover previews and click holds its evidence panel; document links navigate evidence. Root recommends consistency for map entity activation plus explicit reading controls, not making every hyperlink a pin action. The existing 3D exploration contract is recent owner-selected behavior, so changing it to inspection would be a distinct design change.
- Reading must join application-owned explicit state and the existing share/local-persistence/scrubbing path. The card view should receive canonical Purpose and exact-symbol sections from the same GraphFiles the reader uses. Transient hover and current zoom visibility must remain separate from deliberate open/closed choices. Scope for a separately open unpinned card must be explicit; it must not gain a neighborhood pin by accident. Historical files without carried descriptions must stay honest about that absence.
- The branch renderer currently rebuilds card bodies and reruns ordering/measurement on a full render, then starts continuing layout search. That is unsuitable as a naive handler for every row hover: it can replace pointer targets, reorder rows and spend hundreds of milliseconds before any dwell delay. Transient reading needs a controlled geometry update with stable identities/order, correct card heights and wire anchors, plus a restoration path. Pin/navigation changes still need the full retained layout. Tests must measure repeated round trips and moving-pointer journeys at multiple zoom levels after visual review.

Three principles are especially relevant here: show choices people can recognize, keep actions predictable and reversible, and reveal detail without losing the surrounding picture. Hovered text also needs to stay reachable while someone reads it; a modal should open only through an explicit action and return focus when closed. [Usability heuristics](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/ten-usability-heuristics/) · [Hover guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus) · [Dialog guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/)

I’m making three matched zoom variants: scale all text, fold prose in the overview, and fold prose plus an explicit reader for long text. The same cards and pins will let us see what each gains and loses.

Ran terminal command: cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md <<'EOF'

### Guided design comparison checkpoint

- Consulted primary guidance: Nielsen's ten usability heuristics (recognition, visible state, consistency and user control), W3C's hover/focus guidance (reachable, dismissible and persistent content), W3C's modal pattern (explicit focus entry, Escape/close and focus return), and Shneiderman's 1996 overview/zoom/filter/details-on-demand framework. These support design criteria, not a mandated name-click gesture, arbitrary hover delay or zoom threshold. The owner's repeating-motifs idea remains a hypothesis; layout proximity alone must not be presented as source-level evidence of a software pattern.
- Built a new ignored comparison at dist/explorer/probes/reading-zoom with three matched treatments: scale all text; fold prose in the overview; fold overview plus explicit reading for long prose. It keeps the actual native cards, graph content, pin states and both name-click alternatives. Camera range is 0.05–3, with wheel, background pan, slider and matched Overview/Reading size stops. Semantic-fold trial thresholds are 0.8 down and 0.95 up, preserving saved reading choices; these are prototype values, not an owner decision.
- The third treatment limits a long inline passage by rendered height and supplies Read full description, plus a file-level book action for the complete text. A native dialog keeps one ordinary reading scroll surface, with no scrollbars in symbol prose, explicit close/Escape and focus return. Hover visits remain the earlier acknowledged behavior so this comparison isolates zoom/overflow; do not claim the sticky-hover concern is fixed or that this fixed-scope camera implements production layout.
- Build succeeded. Visual/motion inspection, meaningful comparison checks and retained findings are next. The original shared prototype and product page remain intact. The new comparison has not yet been shared or committed as evidence.
EOF

User: >I also owe you more design judgment than a menu of abstract preferences. I’ll explain the relevant UX principles, recommend a small set of promising approaches, and show them working on the same examples—including their failure cases. You should be judging something you can see and use.

I would really sincerely appreciate that. One of the enormous pitfalls with folks using AI coding tools these days is skill atrophy. But at my day job, where usage of AI goes through much more strict approvals (agentic okay, but approving every tool call, me manually copying prompts and responses back and forth from my Obsidian notebook -- the slow and steady careful version of AI use -- rather different than the use I show here in this open-source repo, where I want to encourage LLMs to do their thing), I am **always learning**. 

Our tool should help me learn our tool, but more than that, I'd really appreciate your help in general, in helping me learn and grasp the concepts you're working with. This is especially true if they are UI/UX concepts. From color theory to layout best practices to many other artistic-related ideas, I am nearly clueless to. 

The recent frontier models (Astra and Fable) have been quite terse in user interactions. So terse that it can be hard to understand what is even being intended or communicated. I wish not necessarily for the opposite (verbosity everywhere!) but actual engagement with what I, the user, do and do not know. Just a bit of "know your audience" can likely go a long way to making sure that I can contribute even more meaningfully, and make fewer design mistakes as _built_ experiments, stopping them instead at _thought_ experiments. 

Codex: That distinction is useful: you want enough explanation to participate in the reasoning, rather than having to infer it from my conclusions. I’ll explain unfamiliar concepts when they affect a decision, give a concrete example, and say where I’m confident versus where we need to look.

For example, **semantic zoom** means changing how much detail is shown as you zoom—not just making the same picture smaller. Ordinary scaling turns 13-pixel prose into roughly 6-pixel prose at 45% zoom. We can predict that readability problem before building anything substantial. Folding the prose solves that problem, but creates another: rows and connections move as the card contracts. Whether that transition preserves your bearings is the part we need to see and test.

That is the distinction I should make more often: which approaches we can reject through reasoning, and which deserve a visual experiment. The new comparison uses plain scaling as a baseline; my recommendation is overview folding, with an explicit reader for unusually long text.

"'```markdown
Three principles are especially relevant here: show choices people can recognize, keep actions predictable and reversible, and reveal detail without losing the surrounding picture. Hovered text also needs to stay reachable while someone reads it; a modal should open only through an explicit action and return focus when closed. [Usability heuristics](https://www.nngroup.com/articles/ten-usability-heuristics/) · [Hover guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/content-on-hover-or-focus) · [Dialog guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/ARIA/apg/patterns/dialog-modal/)

I’m making three matched zoom variants: scale all text, fold prose in the overview, and fold prose plus an explicit reader for long text. The same cards and pins will let us see what each gains and loses.

const {chromium,expect}=require('@playwright/test');
(async()=>{
const browser=await chromium.launch({headless:true});const page=await browser.newPage({viewport:{width:1440,height:960}});const errors=[];page.on('pageerror',e=>errors.push(e.message));page.on('console',m=>{if(m.type()==='error')errors.push(m.text());});
const base='http://127.0.0.1:8900/probes/reading-zoom/';const node='packages/engine/src/live-docs/pathfind.ts';
const main=page.locator('.local-focus');const pause=()=>page.waitForTimeout(260);
const zoom=async v=>{await page.locator('#zoom').evaluate((el,v)=>{el.value=String(v);el.dispatchEvent(new Event('input',{bubbles:true}));},v);await pause();};
const state=()=>page.evaluate(()=>{const q=new URLSearchParams(location.search),r=document.querySelector('.local-focus').getBoundingClientRect();return{pins:q.get('pins'),read:q.get('read'),top:r.y,left:r.x,height:r.height,open:document.querySelectorAll('.local-focus .reading-slot.open').length};});
await page.goto(base+'?'+new URLSearchParams({case:'0',policy:'fold',pins:JSON.stringify([[node,'*']])}));await main.waitFor();await pause();
const before=await state();expect(before.open).toBe(29);
for(let i=0;i<8;i++){await page.locator('#overview').click();await pause();expect((await state()).open).toBe(0);await page.locator('#reading').click();await pause();expect(await state()).toEqual(before);}
await zoom(78);expect((await state()).open).toBe(0);await zoom(85);expect((await state()).open).toBe(0);await zoom(96);expect((await state()).open).toBe(29);await zoom(85);expect((await state()).open).toBe(29);

# Live Documentation: working with agents

This is the shared guide for agents in this repository. Vendor-specific instructions point here; keep skills under "'`.agents/skills/`, with vendor locations holding pointers rather than competing copies.

## Purpose and context

Live Documentation turns source files into a map of what they expose and how they connect, so a person can understand software without holding the whole system in their head. Markdown is canonical; the Explorer and CLI are peers rendering the facts the Live Docs carry. Read [the vision](.mdmd/layer-1/vision.mdmd.md) before proposing features.

Current priorities and decisions live in [direction.md](AI-Agent-Workspace/Memory/direction.md); standing preferences in [owner.md](AI-Agent-Workspace/Memory/owner.md); unchosen ideas in [ideas.md](AI-Agent-Workspace/Memory/ideas.md). [The narrative](AI-Agent-Workspace/Memory/narrative.md) tells the workspace'"'s story, and [the decisions log](.mdmd/layer-3/architectural-decisions.mdmd.md) explains consequential choices. Newer owner instructions supersede older ones. Historical certainty is evidence of what was said, not automatically a current decision.

## Collaborate with judgment

- **Be the lead developer.** Own the code and carry authorized work through investigation, implementation, verification and cleanup. Use established intent and evidence to resolve ordinary implementation choices. Debugging, equivalent refactors, justified dead-code removal, and a higher-effort but clearly more correct solution do not need another approval. A problem predating your current context is still ours to understand and fix.
- **Discuss genuine design forks.** Before building on an unresolved choice that changes the product's meaning or behavior, explain concrete alternatives, consequences and your recommendation. First check whether prior decisions or a small investigation already settle it. When experiments are requested, build alternatives for comparison without treating a trial as a production decision.
- **Keep understanding shared.** Explain consequential choices at the owner's level, introducing unfamiliar concepts with concrete examples, particularly in UI/UX. Show how the work fits the whole system. Distinguish established guidance from hypotheses; reject predictable failures through reasoning before building; show matched visual alternatives when spatial judgment is needed. If an explanation is unclear, repair it plainly. Final replies must contain the context needed to understand the result or answer a pending question. Adapt the detail; there is no terseness or verbosity quota.
- **Keep each other honest.** Give evidence, correct false statements and welcome correction. Do not flatter, invent results or estimates, or turn a tentative suggestion into a settled decision. In a disagreement about a picture, the owner's eye decides; for textual facts, explain what the evidence shows.
- **Prefer the simplest complete, correct form.** “A thing should be as simple as possible, *and no simpler*.” Delete duplication and unnecessary machinery while preserving required behavior and distinctions. Choose readable code and boundaries that permit independent improvement; line counts and brevity are not architectural goals. Fix causes instead of disguising them with workarounds. If correct behavior is genuinely unresolved, discuss the wall.

## Preserve continuity

The following startup and recording work belongs to the root agent. Subagents read applicable guidance and their task context without starting another archive cycle.

- **New chat:** read this guide and Memory, including the entire narrative, then the five most recent session summaries in full, oldest to newest (all available if fewer). Finish the immediately preceding session's summary when captures are available and it is missing or incomplete. Maintain the narrative when that session changed the story, and correct contradicted history in place with a dated source note. Follow the [handoff routine](AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/README.md#session-handoff-routine), respecting explicit user overrides. Rank next work against the vision and direction's Next, not merely the latest open ends.
- **As work proceeds:** at each user turn, preserve newly received user messages and preceding root replies not yet recorded. Keep verbatim messages separate from interpretation, use stable turn references, checkpoint consequential work and record outcomes before finishing. Preserve source captures and unique findings. Follow [the recording convention](AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/README.md#recording-as-work-proceeds).
- **After compaction:** resume the same record from its resumption notes and relevant source turns. Do not bootstrap again or treat the latest steering as cancellation of the active task. Summarize the current chat when the owner requests its close-out.
- **Read and report faithfully.** A requested full read means the whole file; a search, sample or summary's claim is not a substitute. State actual coverage. Never extract private reasoning from agent traces. Recover conversation only from explicitly user-facing messages, excluding tool payloads and subagent threads from the root conversation record.

## Stewardship

- **Leave a clean workspace.** Remove your temporary outputs. Investigate questionable files encountered during the work through contents, consumers, git and relevant chat provenance; remove them when no justified purpose remains. Missing chat provenance alone does not prove a file disposable. Preserve source captures and unique findings, and record consequential deletions. This is initiative during the task, not a mandate for an unrelated repository-wide purge.
- **Own delegated work.** Subagents are welcome, up to five within the host's limits. Give bounded scopes and require changed/created files, outcomes and unfinished work in the handback. The root reviews their work, carries forward useful findings and cleans up disposable outputs, including after an interrupted agent. Shared history and Memory maintenance remain the root's responsibility.
- **Use git carefully.** Never bulk "'`git checkout`, `git restore` or `git clean`. Commit when confident in safety and quality under the owner'"'s standing grant. They may push at any moment: never amend or rewrite an existing commit; correct it with the next commit. Write messages an outside reader can understand, without internal option or requirement IDs.

## Documentation and correctness

- **Keep one source of facts.** Everything the Explorer or CLI shows must be derivable from the Live Docs. Grow the format when it cannot carry a needed fact; do not add a private side channel. Consumers read the graph derived afresh from the docs. What a person can learn or do through the UI must also be available through commands or exported data. Agents use those same human-accessible surfaces.
- **Respect generated and authored content.** Never hand-edit a "'`LIVE-DOC:BEGIN` … `LIVE-DOC:END` region; fix the generator. Write authored Purpose and Notes for a new maintainer, from the file'"'s origin in git and the chat record. Cite the relevant summary turn when the origin or decision explains the file. If its purpose cannot be defended, investigate improving or deleting the code. Before deleting an authored document, carry forward still-valid information that lives nowhere else.
- **Distinguish current from historical.** Authored documents identify their status at the beginning. Current docs may cite historical ones for provenance, not depend on them for current facts. Carry old ideas forward as dated history or open questions unless the owner reaffirms them. Name requirements plainly; no requirement-ID schemes. Keep shared memory in the repository, not duplicated in a vendor's private memory.
- **Separate product from workspace conventions.** Read "'`.live-docs.config.json`; product code must not hardcode this repository'"'s "'`.mdmd` root, `layer-4` or `.mdmd.md` suffix. The derived index and history caches are not committed. [The workspace guide](AI-Agent-Workspace/README.md) explains records, artifacts and local output locations.
- **Fix causes, not gates.** Never weaken a valid threshold, exclude a troublesome path, skip a test or add a workaround to obtain a pass. Correct a wrong check and explain the evidence. Test meaningful inputs and learned edge cases; a test that cannot fail proves nothing. Adapters are the product, independent compiler oracles are ground truth, and nothing grades itself. Never trim benchmark expectations to fit the analyzer; block only genuine framework/builtin names.

## Explorer design and verification

- **Preserve orientation.** Following connections, changing scale, opening evidence and returning to a place must keep recognizable subjects and context. Transitions should explain spatial changes. Inspect motion and complete journeys, not only still pictures. Use consistent meanings across scales; current view behavior and open designs belong in [the Explorer design](.mdmd/layer-3/live-documentation-explorer.mdmd.md).
- **Make the picture legible.** Keep readable names, necessary controls, relevant evidence and honest indications of hidden content. Avoid decorative prose and repeated instructions; put optional help in contextual disclosure. Use recognizable icons for repeated actions with accessible names, keyboard access, state and hover/focus explanations. Do not shrink text to make room. Fade the irrelevant rather than boosting the relevant. No emoji in the UI.
- **Keep interfaces coherent.** Blue identifies what an interface offers; green what uses it. In the 2D file drawing, uses enter on the left and offerings leave on the right. Preserve these distinctions across scales while following each view'"'s current rendering design. Prefer a symbol's origin file over a barrel re-export when resolving links.
- **Keep the page self-contained.** The page reads only its own site. Preserve its CSP and no-network checks, use listeners rather than inline event attributes/scripts, and render raw HTML in documentation as text.
- **Look, then protect the behavior.** After an Explorer change, build and use it, inspect screenshots and motion, and save named evidence following [the workspace convention](AI-Agent-Workspace/README.md). Ask why if the Explorer itself was unhelpful for looking. Write browser regressions after visual judgment. Keep layout math in pure modules with Vitest tests; verify DOM behavior with Playwright, not jsdom. Use the design audit to catch collisions and cut-off text over relevant states on this repository and the estate sample.

## Working environment and checks

The devcontainer uses Linux/Bash and the Node version in [.nvmrc](.nvmrc), with npm workspaces defined in [package.json](package.json). The Windows-backed mount is case-insensitive: confirm filename casing through directory listings, not existence checks alone. CLI entry points live under "'`scripts/live-docs/`; source/package responsibilities and current status are documented in the vision and Live Docs.

| Command | Use |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run build` | Type-check/build the engine, Explorer and generator. |
| `npm run live-docs:generate` | Regenerate the mirror and graph after source changes; commit regenerated docs with the code. |
| `npm run live-docs:visualize` | Build the static Explorer for inspection. |
| `npm run safe:commit -- --e2e` | The fullest applicable local verification, including Playwright. |

Run the full applicable local gate after product changes; keep CI'"'s existing default without Playwright. Report failures and environment blockers instead of silently substituting a narrower pass. Run dependency inspection before and after risky edits. Detailed commands, sample builds, audits, oracles and the layout lab are in [internal tooling](.mdmd/layer-2/internal-tooling.mdmd.md); user-facing commands are in [the CLI reference](.mdmd/layer-1/guides/cli-reference.mdmd.md).
EOF

- Primary support for communication/provenance: [October 8 session Turn 12](../10/2026-10-08.1.record.md#turn-12), dated October 9, explicitly rejects hyper-terse ambiguity and makes explanation the agent's responsibility; [October 19 Turn 18](../10/2026-10-19.1.record.md#turn-18) asks for concrete tradeoffs in the final, and says to pursue the obvious elegant answer when reasoning exposes one; [October 8 Turn 5](../10/2026-10-08.1.record.md#turn-5) preserves the origin-based Purpose/Notes workflow. The [September 30 cleanup request](../09/2026-09-30.2.record.md) around line 748 asks initiative during ordinary work, not an unrelated purge.
- Rewrote AGENTS around lead-developer ownership, genuine consequential forks, shared understanding, correctness and continuity. Removed the obsolete low-chatter constraint and blanket repeated-approval framing. Preserved full requested reads, mandatory root startup/recording, no private-reasoning extraction, authored provenance, valid gates/oracles, visual verification, configurable product paths, offline behavior, git safeguards and root ownership of delegation/cleanup. The stale September 'no replacement selected' sentence was removed after verifying the owner's [October 12 Turn 4](../10/2026-10-12.1.record.md#turn-4) declaration that view unification was complete.

Root applied all three. No further substantive omissions or new approval mechanisms were found.
- The focused touch retest now passes: a pinch beginning on a card doubles zoom without changing pins, and subsequent taps independently open/close the file reader and pin the file. That fixes the disposable probe's background-only pinch guard; production code remains untouched.

I rewrote [AGENTS.md](), reducing it from **3,411 to 1,683 words** after a targeted historical audit and a second review.

Its central guidance now brings together what your prompts consistently ask for:

- **Take ownership:** carry authorized work through debugging, implementation, verification and cleanup.
- **Discuss genuine design forks:** first investigate whether prior intent or evidence already settles the choice. Ordinary implementation work does not need repeated approval.
- **Keep understanding shared:** explain unfamiliar concepts with concrete examples, give recommendations and tradeoffs, and show visual alternatives when seeing them matters.
- **Preserve the safeguards:** provenance, honest tests, visual playtesting, continuity and careful git handling remain.

“Low chatter” is removed outright. Continuity is handled through records and checkpoints. Duplicated inventories and command details moved into existing reference documents.

The audit also confirmed that your requests for understandable explanations predate today—for example, [the October 9 exchange](). I should have reconciled that newer guidance with the old constraint.

The [live zoom comparison](http://127.0.0.1:8900/probes/reading-zoom/?case=0&policy=fold&zoom=0.45&pins=%5B%5B%22packages%2Fengine%2Fsrc%2Flive-docs%2Fpathfind.ts%22%2C%22*%22%5D%5D) is also ready. **Overview** and **Reading size** make *semantic zoom* tangible: changing the amount of detail shown, rather than merely shrinking everything. My recommendation is overview folding, restored reading choices when zooming back in, and an explicit reader for unusually long text.

The important thing to judge is whether that change preserves your bearings. The comparison still has the earlier sticky hover behavior; production integration and its final click semantics remain unfinished. I saved a [short explanation of the UX principles and tradeoffs]() alongside the pictures.

Documentation audits and prototype browser checks passed, including repeated zoom cycles, keyboard focus and touch. Committed as `9cdefee9`; the tracked workspace is clean.

User: Thank you for these efforts. I'm reading through your explanation of the UX principles and tradeoffs now. After playing with the linked live zoom comparison, I can confidently say that the threshold for activating the text unwrap is not met. And that's plausibly because I didn't very well ground this. 

Perhaps a designer is growing in me yet.

Look, imagine that I wanted to share with a colleague a view of everything that interacted with _one_ Csharp class. I'd link them to the 2D map, and the first thing that I'd tailor this view to do is center on that **CARD** (I cannot stress enough how "Card" really does carry the semantics of trading/playing cards) such that everything that can be known about the class is shown in one single view (as best as possible). Cutting off the connected neighbors is likely fine, as _zooming interactions are part of the pedagogical experience when interacting with the software_. Indeed, it would not surprise me if one day I asked for shareable links which could perform a pre-timed zoom-out based on a pre-set centered viewport. So if we imagine the idea that the 2D Local Map should, for a typical node (which is drawn as a _card_), maximize the aesthetics and understanding of _just the one centered node_, and then we begin zooming out from there, we might find that the normalization on the single card will have been an extremely valuable standardization step and aesthetic benchmark/anchor/waypoint. 

I imagine the 2D Local Map as a place where a person learns by zooming out. 

I imagine the 3D Local Map as a place where a person learns by zooming in. 

And I imagine shareable links which could traverse both. 

Codex: This changes the design brief in a useful way. The starting point should be a well-composed, centered card that teaches the subject; zooming out then reveals its relationships. I was tuning disclosure against a generic camera scale before establishing what that first card should look like.

I’ll ground the next comparison in that scenario: a real C# class, its card framed as the primary reading surface, with neighbors allowed to extend beyond the viewport. I’ll also check how this fits the current file-based model and record your “2D learns outward; 3D learns inward” distinction separately from the future idea of timed, shareable journeys.

The useful concept here is a **reference view**: a dependable place to return to and understand the subject. “Reading size” should mean that this card is comfortably readable in this viewport; a raw zoom value such as 100% doesn’t guarantee that.

I’ll use the documented payment-service class as the first benchmark, then check a more densely documented class. Your two learning directions also fit different tasks: explaining a known subject can begin close up, while discovering an unfamiliar structure can begin farther away. Zoom interfaces can support movement between those focused and contextual views. [Research overview](https://doi.org/10.1145/1456650.1456652)

User: One more note before I forget it: while I adore the 3D Local Map's choice to turn clusters into things which expand, I don't think we've gone far enough yet. 

Concepts I'm smushing together:
- Learning in the 3D local map is mediated by clicks and zooms
- Clicking on a "ringed node" expands its cluster into something _slightly_ closer to the actual structure it hides
- The 3D local map has performance issues at very very high numbers of nodes

...Have we ever just tried to like... compute a single mesh which engulfs the actual emergent cluster that would have been resolved by a click on a ringed node in the 3D local map? Could a computed mesh which shrink-wraps the shape of the cluster it engulfs be something that feels fun to click on somehow? How could such a thing be styled? Like a cloud? Just as the mesh but perhaps with some warbling internal motion to imply the heisenberg uncertainty inside it? (or -- more practically -- the approximation math hiding it hahaha!)

It might be really delightfully fun to discover these nodes, unbundling them like releasing the canvas wrapping around a peculiarly-shaped flock of balloons, all strung together in odd ways, and sometimes even themselves carrying another mesh, waiting to be popped to be opened up and revealed.

(I also come from a professional game dev background)

Codex: I’ll investigate that alongside the card work. With your game-development background, the useful connection is to **level of detail**: a group can remain recognizable and interactive while its individual contents are withheld.

There are two separate questions to check. One is how to draw a convincing wrapper and make opening it feel good. The other is whether the hidden group can be represented cheaply enough to reduce simulation and interaction costs as well as drawing costs. A single mesh would not automatically solve all three.

I’ll look for earlier experiments in the workspace and check what a ringed node currently represents. That will tell us whether a wrapper can describe a real, stable group or whether neighboring wrappers would claim overlapping sets of files.

- Targeted archive/probe/research/code searches found the owner's October6 proposal for genuine membrane warbling and shrink-wrapping, but no implementation or retained probe of the proposed 3D group envelope. Read that complete user passage in the October6 record around line891. This is a bounded search finding, not proof that no related wording exists anywhere.
- Current 3D scope is a breadth-first neighborhood of retained subjects plus seeds for unreached disconnected components. A ring means that a shown file has connections left out; it does not uniformly identify one disjoint owned cluster. Neighborhoods can overlap. The renderer already uses four shared instance batches (spheres, wires, arrows, rings). The view filters to scoped/revealed nodes before giving them to simulation. Therefore a wrapper's performance case must distinguish fewer prepared/drawn/picked elements from cheaper layout; deriving an exact future silhouette by laying out every hidden member can give back some discovery savings. The unambiguous first visual candidate is a whole disconnected component; recursive refinement inside a connected component needs a grouping policy.
- Consulted Cockburn/Karlson/Bederson's review of focused/contextual interfaces (ACM DOI10.1145/1456650.1456652). It supports describing the user's pedagogical navigation in terms of moving between focus and context, not treating overview-first as a mandatory entry for a shared link to a known class. Card-relative normalization is the root's implementation hypothesis, to be judged on actual content and viewports.
EOF



I found a close ancestor of this idea: in October you described membranes that visibly strain and shrink-wrap their contents. I haven’t found a retained experiment that wraps a 3D discovery cluster in a clickable mesh.

There’s an important distinction in the current implementation: a ring means “this file has connections that aren’t shown.” Some rings mark unopened islands, but ordinary frontier neighborhoods can overlap. Also, spheres and wires are already batched, and only the shown nodes enter the simulation. A wrapper would therefore need to earn its performance benefit beyond reducing scene-object count.

The cleanest first visual experiment is one actual disconnected island, using one frozen layout for several wrapper styles. That lets us judge the reveal without pretending we’ve solved recursive grouping. I’m keeping the centered C# card as the immediate 2D benchmark alongside that investigation.

import fs from 'node:fs';
import { explorerGraphOf } from '../../../../packages/explorer/src/shared/graph';
import { spatialGraphOf } from '../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphData';
import { connectedNeighbours, componentMembers } from '../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphScope';
import { easeSpatialLayout } from '../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/views/forceGraphLayout';
import { getDefaultTuning } from '../../../../packages/explorer/src/client/persistence/local-storage';
const bundle=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('dist/explorer/samples/estate/explorer-data.json','utf8'));
const graph=spatialGraphOf(explorerGraphOf(bundle.graph),()=>true);
const groups=[...componentMembers(connectedNeighbours(graph.nodes,graph.links)).entries()].sort((a,b)=>b[1].length-a[1].length);
const [seed,members]=groups[0];const ids=new Set(members),nodes=graph.nodes.filter(n=>ids.has(n.id)),links=graph.links.filter(l=>ids.has(String(l.source))&&ids.has(String(l.target)));
const started=performance.now();const result=easeSpatialLayout(nodes,links,getDefaultTuning().forceGraph,'connections',{maxTicks:12000});
const output={source:'Standalone estate bundle; largest connected component',seed,files:nodes.length,ticks:result.ticks,still:result.still,layoutMs:performance.now()-started,nodes:result.nodes.map(n=>({id:n.id,name:n.id.split('/').at(-1),x:n.x,y:n.y,z:n.z,symbols:n.publicSymbols.length})),links:result.links.map(l=>({source:typeof l.source==='string'?l.source:l.source.id,target:typeof l.target==='string'?l.target:l.target.id,count:l.count}))};
if(output.nodes.some(n=>"'![n.x,n.y,n.z].every(Number.isFinite)))throw Error('"'Nonfinite layout');
fs.writeFileSync('dist/explorer/probes/cluster-wrap/snapshot.json',JSON.stringify(output));console.log(JSON.stringify({seed,files:output.files,links:output.links.length,ticks:output.ticks,still:output.still,layoutMs:Math.round(output.layoutMs)},null,2));
EOF

### First visual implementations

- Built a new ignored 2D card-reference comparison using canonical data, native card/pin/Markdown helpers, all direct inbound/outbound files and their induced references, with Portrait/Wide compositions and a reading scale measured from the selected card's rendered size. The minimum reading scale is one, so a large card is not made to fit through smaller text. Comments and referenced types remain distinct; a multi-class file stays a file card. Explicit Read card/Connections/Wider stops compare outward learning from the subject. All known direct neighbors remain in the scene even when clipped at the first view.
- Initial looking found that the comparison's controls consumed too much of the reading frame, and a documented three-symbol class overflowed even in the wide composition. Moved secondary controls into an Options popover and the pin tray into the footer, preserving readable type; the complete Processor card now fits the 1440×960 frame. The payment-service portrait fits the smaller viewport when actually available space is used rather than mistaking the preferred outer margin for unavailable space. Overfull modules still remain at reading size with explicit access to full text.
- The 2D probe holds the subject card's center during disclosure transitions and derives each reading pose afresh; it does not add permanent hover offsets. This does not yet solve the previously reported sticky hover policy or implement the production directory-aware layout. Visual checks and meaningful round-trip tests remain to complete.
- Built a separate 3D material/reveal comparison for one actual disconnected component from the standalone estate bundle. Its existing production physics settled the 30 files and 57 reference pairs in416 ticks; preparation measured58 ms on this machine. That is a small cached example, NOT a large-graph performance claim or the exact future placement beside all other components. A padded convex hull with50 vertices wraps the frozen layout; it can bridge empty space and is not a concave shrink-wrap. Three styles share that geometry: translucent skin, taut cage and clouded shell; explicit unwrapping reveals the same file positions and wires. Optional motion is decorative, not measured uncertainty. Recursive grouping is deliberately not fabricated.
- The first preparation call failed with TypeError: pulls is not a function because the helper omitted the production connectivity predicate. Read the actual signature, supplied the same connection-layout predicate as the view and regenerated successfully. A later orchestration input had a syntax error before any file-writing call ran; split the file creation into a patch and rebuilt successfully. Neither failure changed product source.
- Shared new pages: card-reference 769fe729-9177-41af-8641-8ce74a48a6e9 and cluster-wrap c8d7bddb-c3b8-4dd8-882a-4f0942032e49, both on port8900. The integrated-browser query wrapper percent-encoded separators; the probes' defaults recovered the intended initial cases. Existing shared pages and the owner's server are preserved.
EOF



The centered-card comparison is now working with real C# data. The payment-service card presents its purpose, public symbols and recorded type relationships in one frame; the neighboring files can begin offscreen. Portrait and wide compositions let us compare the same information without reducing the text size.

I also have a small 3D wrapper comparison using 30 real files and 57 reference pairs from one estate component. It uses the Explorer’s physics for the frozen positions, then compares three skins over the same hull. The shape stays put when opened, which lets us judge the wrapper and reveal before introducing more complicated unfolding physics or recursive groups.

Largest component:30 files,57 pairs, seed PaymentService/PaymentService.cs, settled416 ticks (58ms locally). Cached snapshot.json has all positions; no runtime physics/performance claim. Three styles share a padded convex hull (50vertices), not concave shrink-wrap: skin, cage, cloud. Cloud was changed from a hard-edged halo to a softer shader; final cloud capture still needed. Need cycle/click/orbit/reload/reduced-motion checks and final source-grounded report.
