const {chromium,expect}=require('@playwright/test');
const ROOT='http://127.0.0.1:8900/probes/';
(async()=>{
 const browser=await chromium.launch({headless:true});
 try{
  const errors=[];const page=await browser.newPage({viewport:{width:1440,height:960}});
  page.on('pageerror',e=>errors.push(e.message));page.on('console',m=>{if(m.type()==='error')errors.push(m.text());});
  const wait=()=>page.waitForTimeout(320),main=page.locator('.local-focus');
  const state=()=>page.evaluate(()=>{const q=new URLSearchParams(location.search),c=document.querySelector('.local-focus').getBoundingClientRect(),v=document.querySelector('#viewport').getBoundingClientRect();return{pins:q.get('pins'),read:q.get('read'),box:[c.x,c.y,c.width,c.height],frame:[v.x,v.y,v.width,v.height],distance:q.get('distance')};});
  for(const viewport of [{width:1440,height:960},{width:980,height:780}]){
   await page.setViewportSize(viewport);
   for(const shape of ['portrait','wide']){
    await page.goto(ROOT+'card-reference/?case=0&shape='+shape);await main.waitFor();await wait();const s=await state();
    expect(s.box[0]).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(s.frame[0]);expect(s.box[1]).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(s.frame[1]);expect(s.box[0]+s.box[2]).toBeLessThanOrEqual(s.frame[0]+s.frame[2]+1);expect(s.box[1]+s.box[3]).toBeLessThanOrEqual(s.frame[1]+s.frame[3]+1);
    expect(Math.abs(s.box[0]+s.box[2]/2-(s.frame[0]+s.frame[2]/2))).toBeLessThan(1);expect(Math.abs(s.box[1]+s.box[3]/2-(s.frame[1]+s.frame[3]/2))).toBeLessThan(1);
    expect(Number(new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('zoom'))).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1);
    expect(await main.locator('.symbol-row[data-symbol="Post"] .reference-facts').innerText()).toContain('request: PaymentRequest');
    const graph=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync('dist/explorer/explorer-data.json','utf8')).graph,file=graph.files['tests/integration/programs/csharp/estate/PaymentService/PaymentService.cs'];
    const expected=[...new Set([file.codePath,...file.inbound,...file.outbound])].sort();
    expect(await page.locator('.node-card').evaluateAll(els=>els.map(e=>e.dataset.id).sort())).toEqual(expected);
   }
  }
  await page.setViewportSize({width:1440,height:960});await page.goto(ROOT+'card-reference/?case=0&shape=portrait');await main.waitFor();await wait();const before=await state();
  for(let i=0;i<8;i++){
   await page.locator('#overview').click();await wait();const c=await state();expect(Math.abs(c.box[1]+c.box[3]/2-(c.frame[1]+c.frame[3]/2))).toBeLessThan(1);
   await page.locator('#wider').click();await wait();await page.locator('#reading').click();await wait();expect(await state()).toEqual(before);
  }
  console.log('Card: four normal-frame fits, complete direct scope, eight exact outward/read returns.');
  let cases=0;for(let c=0;c<6;c++)for(const shape of ['portrait','wide']){
   await page.goto(ROOT+'card-reference/?case='+c+'&shape='+shape);await main.waitFor();await wait();
   expect(Number(new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('zoom'))).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1);
   expect(await main.locator('.symbol-label').count()).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0);
   if(c===2)expect(await main.locator('.card-eyebrow').innerText()).toContain('2 documented classes');
   if(c===3)await expect(main.locator('.probe-purpose')).not.toHaveClass(/open/);
   if(c===4)expect(await page.locator('#case-note').innerText()).toContain('exceeds one reading frame');
   cases++;
  }
  await page.goto(ROOT+'card-reference/?case=0');await main.waitFor();await wait();await main.locator('.local-file-inspect').click();await wait();const choice=new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('read'),pins=new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('pins');
  await page.getByRole('button',{name:'Options',exact:true}).click();await page.keyboard.press('Escape');expect(new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('read')).toBe(choice);
  await page.reload();await main.waitFor();await wait();expect(new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('read')).toBe(choice);expect(new URL(page.url()).searchParams.get('pins')).toBe(pins);
  const book=main.locator('.local-file-read');await book.focus();await page.keyboard.press('Enter');await expect(page.locator('#long-reader')).toBeVisible();await page.keyboard.press('Escape');await expect(book).toBeFocused();
  console.log('Card: '+cases+' content/shape cases, absent docs and multiple classes, independent reading/reload, Options Escape and full reader.');

  await page.goto(ROOT+'cluster-wrap/?style=skin');await page.locator('#scene[data-files]').waitFor();await page.waitForTimeout(400);
  const canvas=page.locator('#scene'),baseline=await canvas.screenshot();
  expect(await canvas.getAttribute('data-files')).toBe('30');expect(await canvas.getAttribute('data-links')).toBe('57');expect(await canvas.getAttribute('data-layout-still')).toBe('true');
  for(let i=0;i<8;i++){await page.locator('#toggle').click();await page.waitForTimeout(720);await expect(canvas).toHaveAttribute('aria-expanded','true');await page.locator('#toggle').click();await page.waitForTimeout(720);expect((await canvas.screenshot()).equals(baseline)).toBe(true);}
  for(const style of ['cage','cloud','skin']){await page.locator('[data-style="'+style+'"]').click();await page.waitForTimeout(200);if(style==='skin')expect((await canvas.screenshot()).equals(baseline)).toBe(true);}
  const r=await canvas.boundingBox();await page.mouse.move(r.x+r.width/2,r.y+r.height/2);await expect(canvas).toHaveCSS('cursor','pointer');await page.mouse.click(r.x+r.width/2,r.y+r.height/2);await page.waitForTimeout(720);await expect(canvas).toHaveAttribute('aria-expanded','true');
  await canvas.focus();await page.keyboard.press('Enter');await page.waitForTimeout(720);await expect(canvas).toHaveAttribute('aria-expanded','false');
  await page.mouse.move(r.x+r.width/2,r.y+r.height/2);await page.mouse.down();await page.mouse.move(r.x+r.width/2+90,r.y+r.height/2+30,{steps:8});await page.mouse.up();await page.waitForTimeout(400);await expect(canvas).toHaveAttribute('aria-expanded','false');
  await page.locator('[data-style="cloud"]').click();await page.locator('#toggle').click();await page.waitForTimeout(720);await page.reload();await page.locator('#scene[data-files]').waitFor();await expect(canvas).toHaveAttribute('aria-expanded','true');await expect(page.locator('[data-style="cloud"]')).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed','true');
  await page.emulateMedia({reducedMotion:'reduce'});await page.locator('#toggle').click();await page.waitForTimeout(100);await page.locator('#motion').check();const still=await canvas.screenshot();await page.waitForTimeout(450);expect((await canvas.screenshot()).equals(still)).toBe(true);
  console.log('Mesh: eight exact pixel returns, material/camera preservation, real shell click, keyboard, orbit without opening, reload and reduced motion.');
  expect(errors).toEqual([]);console.log(JSON.stringify({cardCases:cases,zoomCycles:8,meshCycles:8,errors},null,2));
 }finally{await browser.close();}
})().catch(e=>{console.error(e);process.exitCode=1});

The return journey caught a real calibration bug: after zooming out, “Read card” was measuring the card while some of its reference details were still hidden, then enlarging the restored card too much. I corrected the measurement order; eight outward-and-back cycles now return to the same frame.

That is why the reference view matters beyond aesthetics: it gives us a concrete invariant to protect. The remaining checks cover the content cases, shared state and the wrapper’s reversible reveal.

Ran terminal command: cat >> AI-Agent-Workspace/ChatHistory/2026/11/2026-11-03.1.record.md <<'EOF'

### Verification and preservation checkpoint

- The combined browser check passed: the payment card fits and is centered in both portrait/wide layouts at1440×960 and980×780, the scene contains its complete canonical direct-neighbor set, eight outward/reading cycles return exactly, all twelve content/composition cases preserve exact public-symbol identities, absent Purpose stays absent, multi-class files are identified, separate reading survives reload and Options dismissal, and the full reader returns keyboard focus. The wrapper passed eight exact-pixel open/close returns, material/view preservation, direct shell click, keyboard, orbit without opening, reload and reduced-motion checks, with zero page/console errors.
- The first card round-trip had failed with a790.6px card returning at921.5px: measurement still used overview CSS for type facts and spacing. Synchronizing the full reading presentation before calibration fixed the cause. Another test incorrectly expected lowercase innerText despite the explicit CSS uppercase presentation; the corrected assertion checks the exact underlying two-class text. The exact canonical symbol-name assertions remain case-sensitive.
- Focused follow-ups passed both sides of the hysteresis band's sharing/reload and the shared Connections centering. The current folded/read side is now recorded with the relative scale; a reload no longer changes presentation simply because it lands between the two thresholds. Manual panning cancels the short disclosure hold. Card touch pinch from inside the reading surface doubles relative scale while preserving pins and reading choices.
- Added accumulated-movement cancellation to the wrapper click target, so an orbit out and back to its starting pixel cannot be mistaken for a click. The focused orbit-return/touch checks passed. The final full-text reader keeps uncommented method names and their recorded types, verified with Post/Get/PaymentRequest in the cold estate case.
- Root inspected the final eight card captures and four wrapper captures, including the softened cloud, cold scan and overfull two-class file. Both Gallery sheets are rebuilt and inspected. Removed the unused hard-edged halo from the cloud prototype after the soft material replaced it; no user source or unique evidence was deleted. The current reference probe has improved framing, not a completed native layout or repaired sticky-hover policy.
- The workspace export externally extended to456,677 bytes, SHA-25630b9bc05b41bfa4466b5b133c1966710a838443ed6f0a1e934edf9b6b4728c60, ending with owner Turn16 and an empty Codex prefix. The supplied442,738-byte readonly snapshot remains its exact prefix, so all supplied bytes are preserved without an extra duplicate. EOF



Both comparisons now pass their repeated-return checks. The 2D card returns to the same reading frame, and the 3D wrapper returns to identical pixels after opening and closing.

My current preference is the translucent skin for the wrapper: it reads as a boundary without adding as many lines as the cage. The clouded version is softer and more suggestive, but less precise. Those are visual judgments for you to test; none yet establishes a useful grouping hierarchy or a large-graph speedup.

I’m finishing the evidence and documentation, including your two learning directions and the separate future idea of shareable journeys across them.

This is the November 3–4 active session, not a new chat. Earlier commits: 44a505bf, 08ecee7f, 55a5967a, 05d2b74a and 9cdefee9. Turns 15–16 continued after midnight. The owner rejects the previous unwrap threshold and grounds 2D in a centered, well-composed card, explicitly in the sense of trading/playing cards, teaching a typical C# class in one viewport as far as feasible. Neighbors may be clipped; 2D teaches by zooming OUT, 3D by zooming IN. Timed shareable journeys across perspectives are future ideas. File-pin-all, independent deliberate reading versus retention, no nested text scrolling, a possible explicit long-text dialog, and making the Detail Panel unnecessary remain the direction. Preserve file identity even when the example teaches one class.

Turn 16 proposes meshes wrapping hidden 3D groups, perhaps cloud-like/warbling and recursively opening. The comparisons are ready to try. For the wrapper, I would judge the transition first: whether the revealed files feel like the contents of the object you just clicked, and whether closing it leaves you oriented. The current experiment keeps those positions fixed; more playful unfolding can build on that stable reference.

User: >There’s an important distinction in the current implementation: a ring means “this file has connections that aren’t shown.” Some rings mark unopened islands, but ordinary frontier neighborhoods can overlap.

An extraordinarily important distinction. You're absolutely right.

>The cleanest first visual experiment is one actual disconnected island, using one frozen layout for several wrapper styles. That lets us judge the reveal without pretending we’ve solved recursive grouping. I’m keeping the centered C# card as the immediate 2D benchmark alongside that investigation.

Solid. I played with the 3 options and Translucent skin and Clouded shell are my favorites so far, with Clouded Shell likely being the most favored due to its ability to _reduce_ the visible line density. Its amorphous texturing, unlike the wireframe-like look of Taut cage (and to an extent, Translucent skin), lends it well to reducing visible noise. The only trouble is that it's less fun to "pop" like bubble wrap. Translucent skin gives me that itch to click it and pop it open -- a balloon full of balloons (also potentially full of balloons, and so on) living in a tangled world of bizarre gravity. 

As far as recursive wrapping and performance, I think the focus should be on ensuring that the end user does not lose sight of the actual structure of the softward in the 3D local map, while also not needing to know _every single node on it_ all at once. If you think of it more from an optimization perspective (obscuring nodes which aren't part of the hop chain in the current pin set, etc.) the problem of what to wrap and not wrap may resolve more cleanly. (I also casually envision perhaps a combination? Haven't thought the repercussions of that far enough through though to really recommend). But I can say that your mesh approach is pretty cool! And whether it becomes a helpful tool to safely obscure irrelevant parts of the global structure or whether it becomes a source of tactile delight in discovery is frankly not yet known. The two may not be able to coexist. I'm not yet certain. But I'm finding the mesh wrapper side experiment fun to play with. 

Codex: That gives the two materials different strengths: the cloud helps you read the surrounding graph; the skin makes the object inviting to open. In UX terms, that invitation is a **signifier**—a visible clue about what you can do. We could test a quiet cloud at rest that gains a smooth rim on hover or keyboard focus, without bringing back the cage’s internal lines.

Your optimization framing also gives us a better starting point for membership: preserve the files and paths relevant to the current pins, then examine what remains outside that scope. The important test is whether summarizing those remaining regions preserves their relationship to the visible graph. A cloud that conceals a major connecting route could mislead even if it saves work.

I’ll record this as your current direction, with cloud and skin still useful candidates. The card work remains the immediate integration task; this experiment hasn’t yet chosen between background simplification, discovery, or a combination.

User: >A cloud that conceals a major connecting route could mislead even if it saves work.

Dangerous and true. It then becomes an extraordinarily difficult hill-climbing dance (possibly using lots of subagents) attempting to permute the various levers which cause layout in the 3D local map, such that improper and unwanted overlaps are diminishingly and vanishingly rare. The problem sliiiightly reminds me of a 3D graphics problem I saw a talk about, where there was a ton of dynamic mesh work at play for a character which could wear multiple layers of armor (I think this was that realistic medieval game Kingdom Come: Deliverance?) -- pieces could go under and over one another and were required not to overlap or jut through one another in ways that were visually obviously wrong. 

>The card work remains the immediate integration task; this experiment hasn’t yet chosen between background simplification, discovery, or a combination.

Yep-yep, sorry for the distractions hahaha! Just don't want some of these design ideas to just fall through the cracks and be forgotten about (by me specifically). 

Codex: I’ll preserve these ideas with their uncertainties intact. The armor analogy is useful for intersections and occlusion, though I’d separate those from **misrepresenting the graph**: a layout search can reduce visual collisions, while rules and tests should protect which files a wrapper contains and which connections cross its boundary. Correctness shouldn’t depend on finding a lucky arrangement.

I’ll add the cloud-versus-skin feedback and that distinction to the design notes, then return to the card work.

For the card integration, I’ve confirmed an important constraint: the native map lays out cards using their measured dimensions and connection anchors. Expanding prose must participate in that layout; simply attaching the prototype’s text would leave neighboring cards and wires using stale geometry. I’m carrying that into the integration, alongside your requested faster, non-sticky hover behavior.

Ran terminal command: git commit -m "Document centered card and wrapped cluster explorations"

User: >Correctness shouldn’t depend on finding a lucky arrangement.

True. And yet by a big set of competing layout rule constraints, we somehow find a way to map out a _remarkable_ number of connections in the 2D local map before a backwards-wire is finally detected and the 3D local map button glows. I want to find that, erm, pareto-frontier-like maximum of layout legibility and truthfulness. I suspect, not unlike we were able to achieve on the 2D Local Map, that such a thing exists. 

Codex: Yes—the 2D map is evidence for exactly that ambition. My “lucky arrangement” wording undersold what systematic search over competing constraints can achieve.

I’d frame the 3D goal as finding arrangements where you can understand the most relevant structure with the least visual interference and disruption as you explore. Your Pareto-frontier analogy fits: several arrangements may be good in different ways, with no single one improving every criterion. We should compare those tradeoffs, rather than assume that avoiding overlap alone produces the best picture.

And truthfulness has a perceptual side: the underlying edges can all be correct while the picture still encourages a mistaken reading. That deserves evaluation alongside geometry. I’ll preserve that stronger optimization goal, not just a list of cautions about meshes.
